Do **NOT** open the `.nwb` files directly, with either `h5py` or `pynwb`. \n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository when applicable. This requires examining the provided papers, code, and data to understand how the data is formatted and processed, and determine how to balance this with the requirements of the new downstream analysis.\n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your work in two ways. First, statistics of the converted data and decoder accuracy will be compared to those of an expert-written solution. Second, each decision you make about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. A decision that differs from the expert's is acceptable if it is equally well justified; decisions that are poorly justified will be flagged as concerning or incorrect.\n\n## Reference Information\n\n- Reference **papers**:\n  - The whitepaper about the dataset, \"Allen Brain Observatory: Visual Behavior 2P Technical Whitepaper\", is in the file `/app/whitepaper.pdf`.\n  - A paper using this dataset, \"Behavioral strategy shapes activation of the Vip-Sst disinhibitory circuit in visual cortex\", is in the file `/app/paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `/app/methods.txt`. \n- **Code**:\n  - The Allen data **SDK** is in the directory `/app/code`.\n  - Tutorials about using the data are in the directory `/app/tutorials`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `/app/data`.\n- **Documentation** on using this dataset is at https://allensdk.readthedocs.io/en/latest/visual_behavior_optical_physiology.html. `allensdk` documentation is also downloaded to `/app/allensdk_docs/`.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\n- Collect and convert data under the \"Visual Behavior\" task.\n- Temporally align based on ophys timestamp.\n- Segment each recording session into individual trials based on how they are defined in the experiment. Include both the \"Go\" and \"Catch\" trials, but exclude the \"Aborted\" and \"Auto-rewarded\" trials.\n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n\nNo inputs for this task.\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n\n- Image identity (of the image presented during the non-grey screen), categorical variable. Time-varying.\n- Image change, binary variable. Have value of 1 right after a change in image identity, otherwise 0. Time-varying.\n- Running speed, discretized into five equal percentile bins. Time-varying.\n- Pupil diameter, discretized into five equal percentile bins.  Time-varying.\n- Trial outcome. Static per-trial.\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference papers and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference papers and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference papers and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder.\n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. When you find mistakes, document and fix them immediately. Be critical of results **after every step of processing**. \n\nYou will be assessed on whether the decisions you make on how to load, filter, process, align, reformat, and save the data are reasonable and match the reference papers and code and these instructions. The SDK's built-in trials table was used, since it provides pre-computed trial metadata (outcome, image identity, timing). The full trial window (`start_time` to `stop_time`) is used rather than a fixed window around `change_time`, so that the trial includes both pre-change stimulus flashes and the post-change response window. This enables time-varying output variables (image identity changes mid-trial). Trials are typically ~8s: ~4s pre-change (variable number of stimulus flashes) and ~4.2s post-change (fixed response window).\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Aborted trials (early lick before change), auto-rewarded trials (free reward), and trials without a valid `change_time` are excluded. Trials where `end_idx <= start_idx` (empty window) are skipped. If `stop_time` extends past the recording, the trial is clipped to the end. Sessions with fewer than 2 valid trials are excluded from the final output.\n\nii.\n```python\nvalid_trials = trials_table[\n    (~trials_table['aborted']) &\n    (~trials_table['auto_rewarded']) &\n    (trials_table['change_time'].notna())\n]\n...\nif end_idx > T:\n    end_idx = T\nif end_idx <= start_idx:\n    continue\n...\nif len(trials) < 2:\n    continue\n```\n\niii. Auto-rewarded trials were excluded because the free reward biases the behavioral response. Requiring a valid `change_time` ensures there is a well-defined change point within the trial. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository when applicable. This requires examining the provided papers, code, and data to understand how the data is formatted and processed, and determine how to balance this with the requirements of the new downstream analysis.\n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your work in two ways. First, statistics of the converted data and decoder accuracy will be compared to those of an expert-written solution. Second, each decision you make about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. A decision that differs from the expert's is acceptable if it is equally well justified; decisions that are poorly justified will be flagged as concerning or incorrect.\n\n## Reference Information\n\n- Reference **papers**:\n  - The whitepaper about the dataset, \"Allen Brain Observatory: Visual Behavior 2P Technical Whitepaper\", is in the file `/app/whitepaper.pdf`.\n  - A paper using this dataset, \"Behavioral strategy shapes activation of the Vip-Sst disinhibitory circuit in visual cortex\", is in the file `/app/paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `/app/methods.txt`. \n- **Code**:\n  - The Allen data **SDK** is in the directory `/app/code`.\n  - Tutorials about using the data are in the directory `/app/tutorials`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `/app/data`.\n- **Documentation** on using this dataset is at https://allensdk.readthedocs.io/en/latest/visual_behavior_optical_physiology.html. `allensdk` documentation is also downloaded to `/app/allensdk_docs/`.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\n- Collect and convert data under the \"Visual Behavior\" task.\n- Temporally align based on ophys timestamp.\n- Segment each recording session into individual trials based on how they are defined in the experiment. Include both the \"Go\" and \"Catch\" trials, but exclude the \"Aborted\" and \"Auto-rewarded\" trials.\n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n\nNo inputs for this task.\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n\n- Image identity (of the image presented during the non-grey screen), categorical variable. Time-varying.\n- Image change, binary variable. Have value of 1 right after a change in image identity, otherwise 0. Time-varying.\n- Running speed, discretized into five equal percentile bins. Time-varying.\n- Pupil diameter, discretized into five equal percentile bins.  Time-varying.\n- Trial outcome. Static per-trial.\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference papers and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference papers and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference papers and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder.\n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. Include both the \"Go\" and \"Catch\" trials, but exclude the \"Aborted\" and \"Auto-rewarded\" trials.\n42\t\n43\t### Decoder Inputs:\n44\t\n45\tNo inputs for this task.\n46\t\n47\t### Decoder Outputs\n48\t\n49\t- Image identity (of the image presented during the non-grey screen), categorical variable. Time-varying.\n50\t- Image change, binary variable. Have value of 1 right after a change in image identity, otherwise 0. Time-varying.\n51\t- Running speed, discretized into five equal percentile bins. Time-varying.\n52\t- Pupil diameter, discretized into five equal percentile bins.  Time-varying.\n53\t- Trial outcome. Static per-trial.\n54\t\n55\t## Target Data Format\n56\t\n57\t- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n58\t- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n59\t\n60\t```python\n61\tdata = {\n62\t    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n63\t        [  # list of trials for session 1\n64\t            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n65\t            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n66\t            ...\n67\t        ],\n68\t        [  # list of trials for session 2\n69\t            ...\n70\t        ],\n71\t        ...\n72\t    ],\n73\t\n74\t    'input': [  # list of sessions\n75\t        [  # list of trials for session 1\n76\t            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n77\t            ...\n78\t        ],\n79\t        ...\n80\t    ],\n81\t\n82\t    'output': [  # list of sessions\n83\t        [  # list of trials for session 1\n84\t            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n85\t            ...\n86\t        ],\n87\t        ...\n88\t    ],\n89\t\n90\t    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n91\t    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n92\t\n93\t    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n94\t    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n95\t        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n96\t    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n97\t\n98\t    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n99\t    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n100\t    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n101\t        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n102\t        ...\n103\t    ],\n104\t\n105\t    'metadata': {\n106\t        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n107\t        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n108\t        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n109\t        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n110\t        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n111\t        ...\n112\t    }\n113\t}\n114\t```\n115\t\n116\t### Key Format Requirements\n117\t\n118\t- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n119\t  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n120\t  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n121\t  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n122\t\n123\t- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n124\t  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n125\t  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n126\t    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n127\t    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n128\t    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n129\t  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n130\t  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n131\t  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n132\t\n133\t- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n134\t  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n135\t  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n136\t  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n137\t  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n138\t  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n139\t\n140\t- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n141\t- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n142\t  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n143\t\n144\t- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n145\t- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n146\t\n147\t- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n148\t  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n149\t\n150\t- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n151\t  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n152\t- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n153\t  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n154\t\n155\t- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n156\t  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n157\t  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n158\t  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n159\t  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n160\t  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n161\t\n162\t## Python environment\n163\t\n164\t- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n165\t- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n166\t- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n167\t\n168\t## Decoder Reference\n169\t\n170\t- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n171\t- Optional arguments:\n172\t  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n173\t  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n174\t  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n175\t- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n176\t\n177\t### Purpose\n178\t\n179\tThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n180\t1. Experimental and behavioral variables\n305\t  - Data processing\n306\t  - Data curation\n307\t- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n308\t\n309\t**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n310\t\n311\t**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n312\t\n313\t---\n314\t\n315\t### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n316\t\n317\t**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n318\t\n319\t**Actions**:\n320\t- Map source data elements to target format components:\n321\t  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n322\t  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n323\t  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n324\t  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n325\t  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n326\t  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n327\t  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `/app/code` directory. \n328\t- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n329\t  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n330\t  - Temporal alignment of trials\n331\t  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n332\t  - Brain regions recorded from\n333\t- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n334\t- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n335\t- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n336\t- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n337\t\n338\t**Done when**:\n339\t- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n340\t- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n341\t- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n342\t\n343\t**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n344\t\n345\t---\n346\t\n347\t### Step 6: Script Development\n348\t\n349\t**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `/app/convert_data.py`.\n350\t\n351\t**Actions**:\n352\t- Write all conversion code in the file `/app/convert_data.py`\n353\t- Script should run as **`python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n354\t- Include options:\n355\t  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n356\t  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n357\t  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n358\t- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n359\t- Use the `allensdk` API to interface with the data. Do not open the `.nwb` files directly. \n360\t- Write efficient code:\n361\t  - Vectorize loops\n362\t  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n363\t  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n364\t  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n365\t- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n366\t  - There are no temporal misalignments\n367\t  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n368\t- Write clear, well-documented functions\n369\t- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n370\t- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n371\t- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n372\t\n373\t**Done when**: `/app/convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n374\t\n375\t**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `/app/convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n376\t\n377\t---\n378\t\n379\t### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n380\t\n381\t**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n382\t\n383\t**Actions**:\n384\t1. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_sample_out.txt`\n385\t  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n386\t  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n387\t  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n388\t  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n389\t2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n390\t  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n391\t  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n392\t  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n393\t3. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_sample_out.txt`.\n394\t4. Analyze the output file `/app/verification_sample_out.txt`:\n395\t  a. Verify no errors reported.\n396\t  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n397\t  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n398\t\n399\t**Done when**: \n400\t- `/app/sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n401\t- `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n402\t- Processing plots show no anomalies\n403\t- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n404\t\n405\t**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `/app/sample_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n406\t\n407\t---\n408\t\n409\t### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n410\t\n411\t**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n412\t\n413\t**Actions**:\n414\t1. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n415\t2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n416\t3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n417\t\n418\t**Investigate any issues**:\n419\t- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n420\t\n421\t**Done when**: \n422\t- Decoder training completes without errors\n423\t- Loss decreases over epochs\n424\t- Accuracies are above chance\n425\t- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n426\t\n427\t**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n428\t\n429\t---\n430\t\n431\t### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n432\t\n433\t**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n434\t\n435\t**Actions**:\n436\t1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n437\t2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n438\t3. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_full_out.txt`\n439\t4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n440\t5. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_full_out.txt`. \n441\t6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n442\t7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n443\t8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `/app/verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n444\t9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n445\t\n446\t**Done when**:\n447\t- `/app/converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n448\t- `/app/verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n449\t- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n450\t- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n451\t\n452\t**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `/app/converted_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n453\t\n454\t---\n455\t\n456\t### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n457\t\n458\t**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n459\t\n460\t**Check 1: Output log verification**\n461\tRead through `/app/verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n462\t\n463\t**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n464\t- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n465\t- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n466\t- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n467\t- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n468\t- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n469\t- Document your sanity checks to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n470\t\n471\t**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n472\t- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n473\t- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n474\t- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n475\t- Fix any mismatches. \n476\t- Document your comparisons in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n477\t- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n478\t\n479\t**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n480\t- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference papers. \n481\t- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n482\t- Fix any discovered issues. \n483\t- Document your comparisons in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n484\t\n485\t**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n486\t- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n487\t- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n488\t\n489\t**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n490\t1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n491\t2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n492\t3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n493\t4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n494\t\n495\tWrite a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n496\t\n497\t**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n498\t\n499\t---\n500\t\n501\t### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n502\t\n503\t**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n504\t\n505\t**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n506\t\n507\t**Actions**:\n508\t1. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n509\t2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n510\t3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n511\t4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the papers. \n512\t\n513\t**Done when**: \n514\t1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n515\t2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n516\t\n517\t---\n518\t\n519\t### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n520\t\n521\t**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n522\t\n523\t**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n524\t- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n525\t- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n526\t- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n527\t\n528\t**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\n529\t- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\n530\t- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers' accuracy for each variable\n531\t- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n532\t- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n533\t\n534\t**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n535\t- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n536\t\n537\t**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n538\t1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n539\t2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n540\t3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n541\t4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n542\t5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and papers. \n543\t\n544\t**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n545\t1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n546\t2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n547\t3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n548\t4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n549\t\n550\t**Report all checks and results** to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n551\t\n552\t**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n553\t\n554\t---\n555\t\n556\t### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n557\t\n558\t**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n559\t\n560\t**Actions**:\n561\t- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n562\t  - Key decisions and rationale\n563\t  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n564\t  - Validation results and tables\n565\t  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n566\t- Create a user-facing `/app/README.md` summarizing:\n567\t  - Dataset description\n568\t  - How to load and use the converted data\n569\t  - Output format specification\n570\t  - Key statistics\n571\t- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `/app/cache/` folder\n572\t- Create `/app/cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n573\t\n574\t**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n575\t\n576\t---\n577\t\n578\t## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n579\t\n580\t**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. Neuroscience Data Specifics\n831\t- **Spike times vs. firing rates**: Decide on appropriate binning for spike data\n832\t- **Trial alignment**: Align trials to meaningful events (stimulus onset, movement, etc.)\n833\t- **Time windows**: Choose appropriate pre/post-event windows\n834\t- **Neuron curation**: Choose filtering criteria for the quality of each neuron's recording based on reference Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n\n3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING WHEN APPLICABLE**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference papers and code, except where the decoder input and output specifications or training a neural decoder require otherwise. You will be assessed on the statistics and decoder accuracy of your converted data, and on whether an expert would consider each of your decisions well justified.\n\n4. **USE THE ALLEN SDK**: The data are stored in an AllenSDK cache. You **must use the `allensdk` API, through `VisualBehaviorOphysProjectCache`, to load, read, and manipulate the data**. Do **NOT** open the `.nwb` files directly, with either `h5py` or `pynwb`. \n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository when applicable. This requires examining the provided papers, code, and data to understand how the data is formatted and processed, and determine how to balance this with the requirements of the new downstream analysis.\n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your work in two ways. First, statistics of the converted data and decoder accuracy will be compared to those of an expert-written solution. Second, each decision you make about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. A decision that differs from the expert's is acceptable if it is equally well justified; decisions that are poorly justified will be flagged as concerning or incorrect.\n\n## Reference Information\n\n- Reference **papers**:\n  - The whitepaper about the dataset, \"Allen Brain Observatory: Visual Behavior 2P Technical Whitepaper\", is in the file `/app/whitepaper.pdf`.\n  - A paper using this dataset, \"Behavioral strategy shapes activation of the Vip-Sst disinhibitory circuit in visual cortex\", is in the file `/app/paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `/app/methods.txt`. \n- **Code**:\n  - The Allen data **SDK** is in the directory `/app/code`.\n  - Tutorials about using the data are in the directory `/app/tutorials`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `/app/data`.\n- **Documentation** on using this dataset is at https://allensdk.readthedocs.io/en/latest/visual_behavior_optical_physiology.html. `allensdk` documentation is also downloaded to `/app/allensdk_docs/`.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\n- Collect and convert data under the \"Visual Behavior\" task.\n- Temporally align based on ophys timestamp.\n- Segment each recording session into individual trials based on how they are defined in the experiment. Include both the \"Go\" and \"Catch\" trials, but exclude the \"Aborted\" and \"Auto-rewarded\" trials.\n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n\nNo inputs for this task.\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n\n- Image identity (of the image presented during the non-grey screen), categorical variable. Time-varying.\n- Image change, binary variable. Have value of 1 right after a change in image identity, otherwise 0. Time-varying.\n- Running speed, discretized into five equal percentile bins. Time-varying.\n- Pupil diameter, discretized into five equal percentile bins.  Time-varying.\n- Trial outcome. Static per-trial.\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference papers and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference papers and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference papers and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder.\n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. When you find mistakes, document and fix them immediately. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the papers. \n\n**Done when**: \n1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n\n---\n\n### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n\n**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n\n**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\n- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\n- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers' accuracy for each variable\n- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n\n**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n\n**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and papers. \n\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n\n**Report all checks and results** to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n\n**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n\n---\n\n### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n\n**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n  - Key decisions and rationale\n  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n  - Validation results and tables\n  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n- Create a user-facing `/app/README.md` summarizing:\n  - Dataset description\n  - How to load and use the converted data\n  - Output format specification\n  - Key statistics\n- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `/app/cache/` folder\n- Create `/app/cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n\n**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n\n---\n\n## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n\n**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. ts)\n210\t\n211\t    # ---------------- running speed ---------------------------------------------\n212\t    # 60 Hz encoder trace (already de-wrapped, transient-corrected and 10 Hz low-pass\n213\t    # filtered by the SDK) linearly resampled onto the ophys frame times.\n214\t    rs = ds.running_speed\n215\t    run_t = rs['timestamps'].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n216\t    run_v = rs['speed'].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n217\t    run_ok = np.isfinite(run_v)\n218\t    run_frames = np.interp(ts, run_t[run_ok], run_v[run_ok])\n219\t\n220\t    # ---------------- pupil diameter ---------------------------------------------\n221\t    eye = ds.eye_tracking\n222\t    if eye is None or len(eye) == 0:\n223\t        raise ValueError(f'{ophys_experiment_id}: no eye tracking data')\n224\t    eye_t, eye_d, eye_ok = pupil_diameter_series(eye)\n225\t    if eye_ok.sum() < 100:\n226\t        raise ValueError(f'{ophys_experiment_id}: too few valid pupil frames')\n227\t    pupil_frames = np.interp(ts, eye_t[eye_ok], eye_d[eye_ok])\n228\t\n229\t    # ---------------- trial windows ----------------------------------------------\n230\t    # A trial owns every ophys frame with start_time <= t < stop_time.\n231\t    a_idx = np.searchsorted(ts, sel['start_time'].to_numpy(dtype=float), side='left')\n232\t    b_idx = np.searchsorted(ts, sel['stop_time'].to_numpy(dtype=float), side='left')\n233\t    keep = (b_idx - a_idx) >= 2\n234\t    n_dropped_short = int((~keep).sum())\n235\t    a_idx, b_idx = a_idx[keep], b_idx[keep]\n236\t    outcome = outcome[keep]\n237\t    sel_kept = sel[keep]\n238\t\n239\t    # ---------------- discretisation of the continuous behavioural outputs -------\n240\t    # Quintile edges are computed per session over exactly the frames that end up in\n241\t    # the converted data, so each session contributes ~20 % of its samples to each bin.\n242\t    # Per-session (rather than global) edges are required because pupil diameter is\n243\t    # measured in camera pixels and is not comparable across sessions/rigs; the same\n244\t    # convention is used for running speed so that the two discretisations match.\n245\t    frame_mask = np.zeros(n_frames, dtype=bool)\n246\t    for a, b in zip(a_idx, b_idx):\n247\t        frame_mask[a:b] = True\n248\t    run_edges = np.percentile(run_frames[frame_mask], QUANTILE_PCTS)\n249\t    pupil_edges = np.percentile(pupil_frames[frame_mask], QUANTILE_PCTS)\n250\t    run_bin = np.searchsorted(run_edges, run_frames, side='right').astype(np.int64)\n251\t    pupil_bin = np.searchsorted(pupil_edges, pupil_frames, side='right').astype(np.int64)\n252\t\n253\t    # ---------------- assemble trials --------------------------------------------\n254\t    neural, output, inputs = [], [], []\n255\t    for i, (a, b) in enumerate(zip(a_idx, b_idx)):\n256\t        T = b - a\n257\t        neural.append(np.ascontiguousarray(traces[:, a:b]))\n258\t        out = np.empty((len(OUTPUT_NAMES), T), dtype=np.int64)\n259\t        out[0] = image_idx[a:b]\n260\t        out[1] = is_change[a:b]\n261\t        out[2] = run_bin[a:b]\n262\t        out[3] = pupil_bin[a:b]\n263\t        out[4] = outcome[i]\n264\t        output.append(out)\n265\t        inputs.append(np.zeros((0, T), dtype=np.float32))\n266\t\n267\t    md = ds.metadata\n268\t    info = {\n269\t        'ophys_experiment_id': int(ophys_experiment_id),\n270\t        'ophys_session_id': int(md['ophys_session_id']),\n271\t        'behavior_session_id': int(md['behavior_session_id']),\n272\t        'mouse_id': str(md['mouse_id']),\n273\t        'cre_line': str(md['cre_line']),\n274\t        'targeted_structure': str(md['targeted_structure']),\n275\t        'imaging_depth': int(md['imaging_depth']),\n276\t        'session_type': str(md['session_type']),\n277\t        'equipment_name': str(md['equipment_name']),\n278\t        'project_code': str(md['project_code']),\n279\t        'n_neurons': int(n_neurons),\n280\t        'n_trials': int(len(neural)),\n281\t        'n_trials_dropped_short': n_dropped_short,\n282\t        'ophys_frame_interval_s': float(np.median(np.diff(ts))),\n283\t        'running_speed_quintile_edges': run_edges.tolist(),\n284\t        'pupil_diameter_quintile_edges': pupil_edges.tolist(),\n285\t        'pupil_blink_fraction': float(1.0 - eye_ok.mean()),\n286\t        'frac_hit': float(np.mean(outcome == 0)),\n287\t        'frac_miss': float(np.mean(outcome == 1)),\n288\t        'frac_false_alarm': float(np.mean(outcome == 2)),\n289\t        'frac_correct_reject': float(np.mean(outcome == 3)),\n290\t        'convert_seconds': time.time() - t_start,\n291\t    }\n292\t\n293\t    result = {\n294\t        'neural': neural,\n295\t        'input': inputs,\n296\t        'output': output,\n297\t        'brain_region': str(md['targeted_structure']),\n298\t        'mouse_id': str(md['mouse_id']),\n299\t        'n_neurons': n_neurons,\n300\t        'cell_specimen_ids': cell_ids,\n301\t        'info': info,\n302\t    }\n303\t\n304\t    if collect_debug:\n305\t        result['debug'] = {\n306\t            'ts': ts, 'traces_mean': traces.mean(axis=0),\n307\t            'traces': traces[:min(30, n_neurons)],\n308\t            'image_idx': image_idx, 'is_change': is_change,\n309\t            'run_frames': run_frames, 'pupil_frames': pupil_frames,\n310\t            'run_bin': run_bin, 'pupil_bin': pupil_bin,\n311\t            'run_edges': run_edges, 'pupil_edges': pupil_edges,\n312\t            'run_t': run_t, 'run_v': run_v,\n313\t            'eye_t': eye_t, 'eye_d': eye_d, 'eye_ok': eye_ok,\n314\t            'a_idx': a_idx, 'b_idx': b_idx, 'outcome': outcome,\n315\t            'trial_start': sel_kept['start_time'].to_numpy(dtype=float),\n316\t            'trial_stop': sel_kept['stop_time'].to_numpy(dtype=float),\n317\t            'change_time': sel_kept['change_time'].to_numpy(dtype=float),\n318\t            'is_go': sel_kept['go'].to_numpy().astype(bool),\n319\t            'frame_mask': frame_mask,\n320\t            'stim': ds.stimulus_presentations[\n321\t                ds.stimulus_presentations['stimulus_block_name'] == STIM_BLOCK][\n322\t                    ['start_time', 'end_time', 'image_name', 'omitted', 'is_change']],\n323\t            'licks': ds.licks['timestamps'].to_numpy(dtype=float),\n324\t            'rewards': ds.rewards['timestamps'].to_numpy(dtype=float),\n325\t        }\n326\t    return result\n327\t\n328\t\n329\tdef _worker(args):\n330\t    eid, collect_debug = args\n331\t    try:\n332\t        return convert_experiment(eid, collect_debug=collect_debug)\n333\t    except Exception as exc:                                   # noqa: BLE001\n334\t        return {'error': f'{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}', 'eid': int(eid)}\n335\t\n336\t\n337\t# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n338\t# Diagnostic plots\n339\t# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n340\tdef plot_processing(res, out_path):\n341\t    import matplotlib\n342\t    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n343\t    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n344\t\n345\t    d = res['debug']\n346\t    ts = d['ts']\n347\t    a_idx, b_idx = d['a_idx'], d['b_idx']\n348\t    eid = res['info']['ophys_experiment_id']\n349\t\n350\t    fig = plt.figure(figsize=(26, 30))\n351\t    gs = fig.add_gridspec(7, 3, height_ratios=[1, 1, 1.4, 1.4, 1, 1, 1], hspace=0.45,\n352\t                          wspace=0.22)\n353\t\n354\t    # ---- row 0: whole-session raw streams -------------------------------------\n355\t    ax = fig.add_subplot(gs[0, :])\n356\t    ax.plot(ts, d['traces_mean'],     label='resampled onto ophys frames')\n368\t    ax.set_ylabel('running speed (cm/s)')\n369\t    ax.set_xlabel('session time (s)')\n370\t    ax.legend(loc='upper right')\n371\t    ax.set_title('[2] = fig.add_subplot(gs[row, 1])\n387\t        stim = d['stim']\n388\t        m = (stim['end_time'] >= t0 - 1) & (stim['start_time'] <= t1 + 1)\n389\t        for _, s in stim[m].iterrows():\n390\t            if bool(s['omitted']):\n391\t                continue\n392\t            col = plt.cm.tab20(IMAGE_TO_IDX.get(s['image_name'], 0) % 20)\n393\t            axo.axvspan(s['start_time'], s['end_time'],      axb.set_xlabel('time (s)')\n426\t\n427\t    go_trials = np.where(d['is_go'])[0]\n428\t    catch_trials = np.where(~d['is_go'])[0]\n429\t    zoom_axes(2, int(go_trials[len(go_trials) // 3]), '[3] Example GO trial')\n430\t    zoom_axes(3, int(catch_trials[len(catch_trials) // 2]) if len(catch_trials)\n431\t              else int(go_trials[0]), '[4] Example CATCH trial')\n432\t\n433\t    # ---- ax.set_xlabel('pixels')\n449\t\n450\t    ax = fig.add_subplot(gs[4, 2])\n451\t    counts = [np.sum(d['run_bin'][mask] == i) for i in range(N_QUANTILES)]\n452\t    counts2 = [np.sum(d['pupil_bin'][mask] == i) for i in range(N_QUANTILES)]\n453\t    x = np.arange(N_QUANTILES)\n454\t    ax.bar(x - 0.2, np.array(counts) / mask.sum(), 0.4, label='running')\n455\t    ax.bar(x + 0.2, np.array(counts2) / mask.sum(), 0.4, label='pupil')\n456\t    ax.axhline(0.2, color='k', ls='--')\n457\t    ax.set_xticks(x)\n458\t    ax.set_xticklabels(QUINTILE_NAMES, rotation=30)\n459\t    ax.legend()\n460\t    ax.set_title('[7] fraction of converted samples per quintile bin\\n'\n461\t                 '(should be 0.2 each)')\n462\t\n463\t    # ---- row 5: blink handling and eye ax.plot(d['eye_t'][seg], d['eye_d'][seg], '.', ms=3, color='0.6',\n467\t            label='raw pupil diameter (NaN on likely_blink frames)')\n468\t    bad = seg & ~d['eye_ok']\n469\t    ax.plot(d['eye_t'][bad], np.zeros(bad.sum()), 'rx', ms=5,\n470\t            label='blink / outlier frames (dropped)')\n471\t    seg2 = (ts > d['trial_start'][0] - 5) & (ts < d['trial_start'][0] + 60)\n472\t    ax.plot(ts[seg2], d['pupil_frames'][seg2], '-', lw=1, color='C3',\n473\t            label='interpolated onto ophys frames')\n474\t    ax.legend(loc='upper right')\n475\t    ax.set_title('[8] pupil: blink removal + interpolation onto the ophys timestamps '\n476\t                 '(60 s excerpt)')\n477\t    ax.set_xlabel('session time (s)')\n478\t    ax.set_ylabel('diameter (px)')\n479\t\n480\t    # ---- row 6: change-triggered averages (alignment sanity check) -------------\n481\t    ax = fig.add_subplot(gs[6, 0])\n482\t    dt = res['info']['ophys_frame_interval_s']\n483\t    win = int(round(2.0 / dt))\n484\t    chg_times = d['change_time'][d['is_go']]\n485\t    chg_times = chg_times[np.isfinite(chg_times)]\n486\t    k = np.searchsorted(ts, chg_times)\n487\t    k = k[(k > win) & (k < len(ts) - win)]\n488\t    if len(k):\n489\t        seg = np.stack([d['traces_mean'][i - win:i + win] for i in k])\n490\t        tt = (np.arange(-win, win)) * dt\n491\t        ax.plot(tt, seg.mean(axis=0), color='k')\n492\t        ax.axvline(0, color='r')\n493\t    ax.set_title('[9] change-triggered mean dF/F\\n(peak must follow t=0)')\n494\t    ax.set_xlabel('time from image change (s)')\n495\t\n496\t    ax = fig.add_subplot(gs[6, 1])\n497\t    allout = np.concatenate(res['output'], axis=1)\n498\t    ax.bar(np.arange(len(IMAGE_NAMES)),\n499\t           [np.mean(allout[0] == i) for i in range(len(IMAGE_NAMES))])\n500\t    ax.set_xticks(np.arange(len(IMAGE_NAMES)))\n501\t    ax.set_xticklabels(IMAGE_NAMES, rotation=90)\n502\t    ax.set_title('[10] image_identity distribution in converted data')\n503\t\n504\t    ax = fig.add_subplot(gs[6, 2])\n505\t    ax.bar(np.arange(4), (frac change='\n509\t                 f'{allout[1].mean():.3f})')\n510\t\n511\t    fig.suptitle(f'Conversion diagnostics: ophys_experiment_id {eid} '\n512\t fontsize=16)\n515\t    fig.savefig(out_path, dpi=90, bbox_inches='tight')\n516\t    plt.close(fig)\n517\t    print(f'  wrote {out_path}', flush=True)\n518\t\n519\t\n520\t# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n521\t# Main\n522\t# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n523\tdef main():\n524\t    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n525\t    ap.add_argument('outfile')\n526\t    ap.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', default=True)\n527\t    ap.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true')\n528\t    ap.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true')\n529\t    ap.add_argument('--workers', type=int, default=24)\n530\t    args = ap.parse_args()\n531\t\n532\t    t_all = time.time()\n533\t    cache = get_cache()\n534\t    meta = select_experiments(cache)\n535\t    eids = list(meta.index)\n536\t    print(f'{len(eids)} experiments selected '\n537\t          f'(active behaviour, project_code=VisualBehavior, locally available)')\n538\t    if args.sample:\n539\t        eids = eids[:2]\n540\t        print(f'--sample: restricting to {eids}')\n541\t    n_debug = 2 if args.show_processing else 0\n542\t    print(f'loading with {args.workers} workers ...', flush=True)\n543\t\n544\t    t0 = time.time()\n545\t    jobs = [(eid, i < n_debug) for i, eid in enumerate(eids)]\n546\t    results = []\n547\t    with Pool(min(args.workers, max(1, len(jobs)))) as pool:\n548\t        for i, res in enumerate(pool.imap(_worker, jobs, chunksize=1)):\n549\t            if 'error' in res:\n550\t                print(f'  [{i + 1}/{len(jobs)}] SKIP {res[\"eid\"]}: {res[\"error\"]}',\n551\t                      flush=True)\n552\t            else:\n553\t                nfo = res['info']\n554\t                print(f'  [{i + 1}/{len(jobs)}] {nfo[\"ophys_experiment_id\"]} '\n555\t                      f'{nfo[\"session_type\"]:<18s} mouse {nfo[\"mouse_id\"]:>7s} '\n556\t                      f'neurons {nfo[\"n_neurons\"]:>4d} trials {nfo[\"n_trials\"]:>4d} '\n557\t                      f'({nfo[\"convert_seconds\"]:.1f}s)', flush=True)\n558\t            results.append(res)\n559\t    t_load = time.time() - t0\n560\t    print(f'conversion of {len(jobs)} sessions took {t_load:.1f}s '\n561\t          f'({t_load / max(1, len(jobs)):.2f}s/session wall clock)')\n562\t\n563\t    ok = [r for r in results if 'error' not in r]\n564\t    skipped = [r for r in results if 'error' in r]\n565\t\n566\t    if args.show_processing:\n567\t        for res in ok[:n_debug]:\n568\t            plot_processing(\n569\t                res, f'processing_{res[\"info\"][\"ophys_experiment_id\"]}.png')\n570\t\n571\t    # ---- assemble the final dictionary -----------------------------------------\n572\t    subjects, brain_regions = [], []\n573\t    for r in ok:\n574\t        if r['mouse_id'] not in subjects:\n575\t            subjects.append(r['mouse_id'])\n576\t    for r in ok:\n577\t        if r['brain_region'] not in brain_regions:\n578\t            brain_regions.append(r['brain_region'])\n579\t\n580\t    data = {\n581\t        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in ok],\n582\t        'input': [r['input'] for r in ok],\n583\t        'output': [r['output'] for r in ok],\n584\t        'subjects': subjects,\n585\t        'subject_idx': np.array([subjects.index(r['mouse_id']) for r in ok],\n586\t                                dtype=np.int64),\n587\t        'brain_regions': brain_regions,\n588\t        'brain_region_idx': [\n589\t            np.full(r['n_neurons'], brain_regions.index(r['brain_region']),\n590\t                    dtype=np.int64) for r in ok],\n591\t        'input_names': [],\n592\t        'output_names': OUTPUT_NAMES,\n593\t        'output_values': OUTPUT_VALUES,\n594\t    }\n595\t\n596\t    dts = np.array([r['info']['ophys_frame_interval_s'] for r in ok])\n597\t    n_trials = sum(len(r['neural']) for r in ok)\n598\t    n_neurons = sum(r['n_neurons'] for r in ok)\n599\t    n_timepoints = sum(int(t.shape[1]) for r in ok for t in r['neural'])\n600\t\n601\t    data['metadata'] = {\n602\t        'task_description': (\n603\t            'Allen Brain Observatory Visual Behavior 2P: head-fixed mice perform a '\n604\t            'go/no-go visual change-detection task while 2-photon calcium imaging is '\n605\t            'performed in visual cortex. A series of natural images is flashed (250 ms '\n606\t            'on / 500 ms gray, 5% of non-change flashes omitted) and the mouse earns a '\n607\t            'water reward by licking within 150-750 ms of a change in image identity. '\n608\t            'Each session is segmented into the experiment-defined Go (real change) and '\n609\t            'Catch (sham change) trials; Aborted and Auto-rewarded trials are excluded. '\n610\t            'The decoder receives no external inputs and predicts, from population dF/F '\n611\t            'alone, five categorical variables: (0) image_identity -- which of the 16 '\n612\t            'natural images is currently being shown, held through the gray screen and '\n613\t            'through omitted flashes; (1) image_change -- 1 for every frame in the '\n614\t            '750 ms image-presentation interval that begins with a change in image '\n615\t            'identity; (2) running_speed_bin -- running speed discretised into 5 equal '\n616\t            'percentile (quintile) bins; (3) pupil_diameter_bin -- pupil diameter '\n617\t            'discretised into 5 equal percentile bins; (4) trial_outcome -- '\n618\t            'hit/miss/false_alarm/correct_reject, constant within a trial.'),\n619\t        'time_bin_size': float(np.mean(dts) * 1000.0),\n620\t        'temporal_alignment_event': (\n621\t            'Ophys (2-photon) frame timestamps. Every data stream is placed on the '\n622\t            'native ophys timestamp grid of its session: dF/F and its timestamps are '\n623\t            'used as recorded, the 60 Hz running trace and the ~30 Hz pupil trace are '\n624\t            'linearly interpolated onto the ophys frame times, and the stimulus and '\n625\t            'trial variables are evaluated at those same frame times. {n_trials}')\n660\t    print(f'timepoints (total)  : {n_timepoints}')\n661\t    print(f'time bin size (ms)  : {data[\"metadata\"][\"time_bin_size\"]:.4f} '\n662\t          f'(sd     vals, counts = np.unique(allout[i], return_counts=True)\n667\t        frac = counts / counts.sum()\n668\t        print(f'output {i} {name}: ' +\n669\t              ',
